# Superhuman Research and the Paranormal: A Comprehensive, Declassified Reference

A general, global reference on government and academic programs investigating superhuman
capacities, including remote viewing, telepathy, psychokinesis, anomalous cognition, and
related phenomena. Covers declassified documents, canonical works, key figures, evaluations,
and full source references.

Declassified documents: 1,809. Scope: global. Status: declassified and published.

---

## Overview

Across the Cold War and beyond, multiple governments and academic institutions investigated
whether human beings possess superhuman or anomalous capacities. These efforts ranged from
classified military intelligence programs to peer-reviewed laboratory experiments. This document
is a general, global reference, not focused on any single agency.

---

## Timeline of Programs

1930s - J.B. Rhine founds experimental parapsychology at Duke University (ESP cards, dice tests).
1970 - CIA funds SCANATE (scan by coordinate), earliest US remote viewing research.
1972 - Stanford Research Institute (SRI) research begins under Russell Targ and Harold Puthoff.
1974 - Pat Price views Semipalatinsk Soviet nuclear test site (PNUTS).
1977 - Army GONDOLA WISH evaluates adversary applications of remote viewing.
1978 - GRILL FLAME (Detachment G) established at Fort Meade, Maryland.
1979 - SRI integrated into GRILL FLAME; PEAR lab opens at Princeton.
1981 - Dozier kidnapping; remote viewers tasked (no credited success).
1983 - CENTER LANE (ICLP) redesignation; Controlled Remote Viewing (CRV) developed.
1984 - National Academy of Sciences review (unfavorable); Jack Anderson leaks program existence.
1984 - Mars remote viewing session (May 22, training exercise).
1985 - SUN STREAK under DIA after Army funding ends.
1986 - Hunt for Gadhafi before Libya bombing.
1988 - National Academy Press, Enhancing Human Performance; Higgins Lebanon session.
1989 - Libyan chemical weapons session.
1991 - STAR GATE under SAIC; SAP to LIMDIS.
1994 - Plutonium in North Korea tasking; no notable success.
1995 - AIR evaluation; program terminated; CIA concludes no ESP data guided operations.
2000 - CREST declassification begins; Mars transcript released.
2000s onward - Internet folklore, commercial remote viewing training, continued skepticism.

---

## United States

CIA SCANATE (1970 onward) funded early research. SRI (1972-1986) led by Targ and Puthoff,
using subjects including Ingo Swann and Pat Price. Army INSCOM GRILL FLAME (1978-1983) at
Fort Meade. CENTER LANE (1983-1985) developed CRV. SUN STREAK (1985-1991) under DIA.
STAR GATE (1991-1995) under SAIC, directed largely by Edwin May. Other entities: Naval and
Air Force human performance research, Army Research Institute, AMSAA, NRC evaluations.
Leaked by Jack Anderson in April 1984. Patrons: Senator Claiborne Pell and Representative Charles Rose.

Soviet scare: US intelligence concluded USSR engaged in psychotronic research, spending roughly
60 million rubles per year by 1970 and over 300 million by 1975, with 300+ personnel. This
drove US funding of SCANATE and SRI research.

---

## Soviet Union and Russia

Psychotronics, the Soviet term for psychic and mind-control research, was pursued by the KGB,
GRU, and military research institutes. Focus areas included telepathy, psychokinesis, psychic
spying, mind control, and psychotronic weapons. Much remains classified. Western knowledge comes
from defectors, open literature, and US assessments. Post-Soviet interest continued in non-lethal
and information weapons, with some research moving to civilian institutes.

---

## China

1980s state-sponsored parapsychology and Qigong research. Studies at Beijing and other institutes
claimed extraordinary human body functions: telepathy, clairvoyance, psychokinesis, external qi.
Documented in Western surveys, including a declassified CIA document on parapsychological research
in the People's Republic of China. Later skepticism and official downplaying followed; research
largely discontinued or moved underground.

---

## Other Nations

United Kingdom: limited Ministry of Defence interest, mostly following the US lead; academic
parapsychology at Edinburgh and Northampton.
India: DRDO and academic interest in consciousness and yoga research; limited declassified detail.
Israel, Germany, France: sporadic academic and defense interest; no major declassified programs
comparable to STARGATE.
North Korea: US remote viewers tasked to find plutonium in 1994; no notable success.

---

## Academic and Civilian Research

Rhine Research Center at Duke University: J.B. Rhine founded experimental parapsychology in the
1930s, ESP cards, dice tests, coined ESP and psychokinesis.
PEAR Lab (Princeton Engineering Anomalies Research, 1979-2007): Robert G. Jahn and Brenda Dunne,
REG experiments, claims of small but statistically significant effects, closed 2007.
Ganzfeld experiments: sensory deprivation paradigm for telepathy; meta-analyses claimed effects,
critics disputed methodology, modern replications generally null.
Maimonides Medical Center dream-telepathy studies (Ullman and Krippner, 1970s).
Global Consciousness Project (Roger Nelson, Princeton): REG network coinciding with global events.

---

## Techniques

Coordinate Remote Viewing (CRV): viewer given geographic coordinates or sealed envelope, reports
raw perceptions.
Extended Remote Viewing (ERV): hybrid relaxation and meditative method.
Written Remote Viewing (WRV): channeling plus automatic writing, introduced 1988, controversial.
Ganzfeld: sensory deprivation for telepathy testing.
REG and PK: random event generator experiments for psychokinesis.
Distant staring and presentiment: physiological response to unseen observation.

A typical session: a monitor cues the subject, records perceptions, moves the subject in time and
space, and keeps the subject deep. The subject reports raw sensory impressions, not analysis.
Transcripts are matched to targets after the fact by analysts.

---

## Key Figures

United States: Ingo Swann (OT VII Scientologist, artist, co-developed CRV); Harold Puthoff
(physicist, ex-NSA, SRI lead); Russell Targ (physicist, SRI co-lead); Pat Price (early subject,
Semipalatinsk); Joe McMoneagle (retired MI officer, 150 targets, Legion of Merit, left 1984);
Edwin May (SAIC-era director); Paul H. Smith (viewer, author); Jessica Utts (statistician, AIR
positive); Ray Hyman (psychologist, CSICOP, AIR negative); Robert G. Jahn and Brenda Dunne (PEAR);
J.B. Rhine (father of experimental parapsychology); Jack Anderson (columnist who leaked the program).

Political patrons: Senator Claiborne Pell (D-RI), Representative Charles Rose (D-NC).

---

## Canonical Works

Books: Paul H. Smith, Reading the Enemy's Mind (2005); Joseph McMoneagle, The Stargate Chronicles
(2002) and Mind Trek (1997); Jim Schnabel, Remote Viewers (1997); Russell Targ and Harold Puthoff,
Mind-Reach (1977); Ingo Swann, Penetration (1998); Annie Jacobsen, Phenomena (2017); Jon Ronson,
The Men Who Stare at Goats (2004); Robert G. Jahn and Brenda Dunne, Margins of Reality (1987);
J.B. Rhine, Extra-Sensory Perception (1934).

Reports: AIR, An Evaluation of Remote Viewing: Research and Applications (September 29, 1995);
NAS/NRC evaluation for Army Research Institute (1984); National Academy Press, Enhancing Human
Performance (1988).

Films: The Men Who Stare at Goats (2009); Third Eye Spies (2019).

Related institutions: Monroe Institute (Gateway Process, hemi-sync); SRI International; SAIC;
PEAR Lab (Princeton, 1979-2007); Rhine Research Center (Duke).

---

## Notable Sessions and Claims

1974: Pat Price described an airfield with a gantry and crane at given coordinates, matching the
Soviet nuclear test site at Semipalatinsk (PNUTS). Most other data incorrect or unevaluable.
Tu-95 crash in Africa: viewer allegedly located wreckage within several miles.
1979: Soviet submarine under construction; predicted 18-20 missile tubes plus flat aft; later sighted
with 24 and 20 tubes plus flat deck after 120 days.
1981: Kidnapping of BG James L. Dozier (Red Brigades, Italy); freed by Italian police; psychics not credited.
1986: Hunt for Gadhafi before Libya bombing; Gadhafi not injured.
1988: COL William Higgins in Lebanon; viewer named a building and village.
1989: Libyan chemical weapons; viewer named ship Patua/Potua; actual ship Batato.
KGB colonel in South Africa: pocket-calculator communications device (claimed).
Gulf War: Saddam Hussein whereabouts (never independently verified).
1994: Plutonium in North Korea: no notable success.
SCUD missiles, bio/chem programs, underground tunnels (claimed).
Mars session (1984): training exercise; subject described ancient Martian civilization.

Eight martini results: so mind-boggling everyone drinks eight martinis to recover.

---

## Evaluations

1984: NAS/NRC for Army Research Institute: unfavorable.
1988: Enhancing Human Performance (National Academy Press).
1995: AIR contracted by CIA. Jessica Utts: statistically significant effect in lab; psychics about
15 percent accurate. Ray Hyman (CSICOP): negative assessment. Final AIR recommendation: terminate
STAR GATE. CIA conclusion: no ESP data ever guided an intelligence operation.
Mainstream science treats RV and ESP as unproven; replication failures; methodological critiques
including monitor influence, post-hoc matching, and vague descriptions.

---

## Legacy

No verified intelligence product credited by CIA or DIA. Methodological legacy: structured RV
protocols, coordinate cueing, transcript-based evaluation. Cultural legacy: internet folklore (Mars
session), commercial remote viewing training (Farsight Institute, RVIS), skepticism (CSICOP/CSI).
Academic parapsychology lab studies continue; mainstream science remains skeptical.

---

## Related Programs (Mind Control and Psychokinesis)

MKULTRA (1953-1973): CIA illegal human experimentation program to develop procedures for
mind control, interrogation, and behavioral modification, using LSD, sensory deprivation,
hypnosis, and other methods. Most records destroyed in 1973; details emerged via congressional
and journalistic investigations. Related to superhuman research insofar as it explored
control of mental states.

NSA psychokinesis research: declassified NSA documents (e.g., NSA-RDP96X00790R000100010041-2)
address psychokinesis and remote viewing as potential SIGINT/COMSEC threats, and review
Soviet parapsychology including Vasilyev's telepathy work.

Soviet Vasilyev telepathy: early Soviet research on telepathy cited in Western assessments.

- GWU National Security Archive STARGATE doc 57: https://nsarchive2.gwu.edu/NSAEBB/NSAEBB438/docs/doc_57.pdf

## Modern Follow-Up Research

Post-declassification academic work continues. Examples: Escola-Gascon et al. (2023), follow-up
on CIA remote viewing experiments published in Brain and Behavior; the sheep-goat effect
(believers score higher than non-believers on psi tests); ongoing Ganzfeld and presentiment
studies. Mainstream science remains skeptical due to replication failures.

## Methodology

1. Scope note: this index covers CREST items whose title or metadata mentions remote viewing or stargate (1,809 docs). Broader parapsychology, psychotronics, and MKULTRA records are referenced in the Related Programs section but not individually indexed.
2. Queried the Internet Archive mirror of the CIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room (collection:
   ciareadingroom) for all items whose title or metadata mentions remote viewing or stargate.
3. Deduplicated by archive identifier, yielding 1,809 unique documents.
4. For each document, fetched its archive.org metadata record and extracted the released OCR text
   (truncated to about 160 characters) as a short description.
5. Generated this index with direct links to both the archive.org mirror and the original CIA PDF.

Because the CIA reading-room site is periodically under maintenance, the Internet Archive mirror
is the most reliable way to read these documents.

- GlobalSecurity: https://www.globalsecurity.org/intell/systems/stargate.htm

Key documents (CREST):
- CIA-RDP96-00788R001900760001-9 - MARS EXPLORATION (May 22, 1984)

- CIA-RDP96-00789R002800180001-2 - STAR GATE project overview

Books:
- Paul H. Smith, Reading the Enemy's Mind (2005)
- Joseph McMoneagle, The Stargate Chronicles (2002); Mind Trek (1997)
- Jim Schnabel, Remote Viewers (1997)

- Ingo Swann, Penetration (1998)
- Annie Jacobsen, Phenomena (2017)
- Jon Ronson, The Men Who Stare at Goats (2004)
- Robert G. Jahn and Brenda Dunne, Margins of Reality (1987)
- J.B. Rhine, Extra-Sensory Perception (1934)

Reports:
- AIR, An Evaluation of Remote Viewing: Research and Applications (Sep 29, 1995)
- NAS/NRC evaluation for Army Research Institute (1984)
- National Academy Press, Enhancing Human Performance (1988)

Media:
- Third Eye Spies (2019), documentary on SRI remote viewing
- The Men Who Stare at Goats (2009), comedy inspired by the program

Journalism:
- The Guardian (Jun 5, 2003), The remote viewers
- Jack Anderson column (Apr 1984), first public disclosure

---

## Disclaimer

These documents are publicly released, declassified records. They are reproduced and indexed here
for research, historical, and educational purposes. The claims within, such as psychic remote
viewing of Mars, are unverified and in many cases contradict established science. The documents
are evidence of what was reported under protocol, not evidence of the phenomena themselves.
The CIA's own conclusion in 1995 was that no ESP data was ever used to guide an intelligence
operation.
